     Plan: fill out _docs/plan.md for "micro git"

     Context

     _docs/AGENTS.md says plan.md is "what the app does: features, endpoints,
     tables, rules". It is currently empty (0 bytes). The other docs (tasks.md,
     design-system.md, process.md) are left over from a previous project
     (BudgetControl, tenge, expenses), so plan.md is the first doc that defines
     the new project. Stack is fixed by AGENTS.md: Go + net/http (1.22
     patterns), pgx, bcrypt, JWT cookie, Neon Postgres, React + TS + shadcn.

     Deadline: one week, university project. So: a tiny git-hosting site
     (GitHub-lite). Real git object model on the backend, commits made through
     the web UI, browsing UI on the frontend. No clone/push protocol.

     After approval, the content below is written to _docs/plan.md (nothing else
     is edited unless the user asks).

     Proposed content of _docs/plan.md

     1. What it is

     micro git: users create repositories, commit file changes from the browser,
     and browse branches, history, files and diffs. Backend mimics git's
     content-addressed storage (blob / tree / commit objects, branch refs).
     Frontend mimics a GitHub repo page.

     2. Core model (the "git" part)

     - Objects use git's exact serialization and SHA-1 (crypto/sha1, stdlib):
       sha1("<type> <len>\0" + body). Blob = file bytes; tree = sorted entries
       mode name\0 20-byte-oid; commit = tree, parent*, author, committer, blank
       line, message.
       - Payoff: oids match real git hash-object, so tests can use known vectors
         (empty blob e69de29b…5391, "test content\n" → d670460b…e3e4) and we
         can say "it is real git format" in the demo.
     - Objects are immutable and de-duplicated per repo (INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO
       NOTHING).
     - A branch is a ref: name → commit oid. Committing = write blobs, rebuild
       only the changed trees bottom-up, write commit, move the ref
       (compare-and-swap on the expected tip, else 409).
     - Branch names: [A-Za-z0-9._-]+, no / (keeps /tree/{ref}/{path...}
       unambiguous).

     3. Features (MVP)

     1. Register / login / logout (username + password, JWT in httpOnly cookie).
     2. Create / list / view / delete repos. Public read for everyone (no login
        needed to browse), write only for the owner.
     3. Commit from the web: edit or create files, delete files, commit message,
        target branch. Multiple files per commit.
     4. Browse: file tree at any branch or commit, file viewer, branch switcher.
     5. History: commit list per branch (first-parent walk, paginated), commit
        detail with per-file line diff.
     6. Branches: list, create from another branch, delete (not the default).

     Stretch, cut in this order if late: fast-forward merge of a branch into
     another; compare two branches; render README.md; rename repo.
     Out of scope: clone/push/fetch protocol, pack files, real merges and
     conflict resolution, tags, forks, PRs, issues, SSH keys, binary files,
     private repos, orgs.

     4. Tables (backend/db/schema.sql)

     ┌─────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
     ┐
     │  table  │                            columns
     │
     ├─────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
     ┤
     │ users   │ id, username (unique, case-insensitive, [a-z0-9-]{3,32}),
     │
     │         │ password_hash, created_at
     │
     ├─────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
     ┤
     │ repos   │ id, owner_id→users, name (unique per owner, case-insensitive),
     │
     │         │  description, default_branch (default main), created_at
     │
     ├─────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
     ┤
     │ objects │ repo_id→repos (cascade), oid char(40), type (blob/tree/commit,
     │
     │         │  CHECK), data bytea (the raw git body); PK (repo_id, oid)
     │
     ├─────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
     ┤
     │ refs    │ repo_id→repos (cascade), name, oid; PK (repo_id, name)
     │
     └─────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
     ┘

     Objects store the raw serialized body (like an uncompressed loose object);
     parsing happens in Go. No separate commits/trees tables (log = walk
     parents, capped at 50 per page, fine at this scale).

     5. Endpoints (stdlib patterns, JSON errors {"error": ...})

     method + path: GET /health
     auth: –
     notes:
     ────────────────────────────────────────
     method + path: POST /auth/register, POST /auth/login, POST /auth/logout,
     GET 
     /auth/me
     auth: – / cookie
     notes: 409 username taken, 400 password < 8
     ────────────────────────────────────────
     method + path: GET /repos
     auth: –
     notes: ?owner= filter
     ────────────────────────────────────────
     method + path: POST /repos
     auth: yes
     notes: {name, description}; 409 duplicate; repo starts empty, no branches
     ────────────────────────────────────────
     method + path: GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}
     auth: –
     notes: includes default branch, empty flag
     ────────────────────────────────────────
     method + path: DELETE /repos/{owner}/{repo}
     auth: owner
     notes: 403 otherwise
     ────────────────────────────────────────
     method + path: GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/branches
     auth: –
     notes: name + tip oid
     ────────────────────────────────────────
     method + path: POST /repos/{owner}/{repo}/branches
     auth: owner
     notes: {name, from}; 409 exists
     ────────────────────────────────────────
     method + path: DELETE /repos/{owner}/{repo}/branches/{branch}
     auth: owner
     notes: 400 for default branch
     ────────────────────────────────────────
     method + path: GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/commits?ref=&page=
     auth: –
     notes: list: oid, message, author, time
     ────────────────────────────────────────
     method + path: GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/commits/{oid}
     auth: –
     notes: files changed + diff hunks vs first parent
     ────────────────────────────────────────
     method + path: GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/tree/{ref}/{path...}
     auth: –
     notes: directory entries (ref = branch or commit oid)
     ────────────────────────────────────────
     method + path: GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/blob/{ref}/{path...}
     auth: –
     notes: file content, size
     ────────────────────────────────────────
     method + path: POST /repos/{owner}/{repo}/commits
     auth: owner
     notes: {branch, expected_tip, message, changes:[{path, content}|{path, 
     delete:true}]} → new commit; 409 if tip moved

     6. Rules

     - Path: no leading /, no ./../empty segments, no .git segment, ≤ 255 chars.
       A path may not be both a file and a directory (400).
     - Limits: file ≤ 1 MiB, UTF-8 text only (400 otherwise), ≤ 50 changes per
       commit, message 1–500 chars, repo name [A-Za-z0-9._-]{1,100}.
     - A commit that changes nothing (same content / deleting a missing file) →
       400 "no changes". Deleting the last file of a directory removes the
       directory (git has no empty trees).
     - First commit on an empty repo has no parent and creates the branch;
       expected_tip is empty in that case.
     - Author = logged-in user; timestamp from an injected clock (testable).
     - Diff: line-based (LCS) computed server-side; files over 1 MiB can't exist
       so no extra cap; response = per file status (added/modified/deleted) +
       hunks with +/-/context lines.
     - Every write endpoint: no token → 401; non-owner → 403.

     7. Frontend (design-system.md styling; money/budget rules there to be 
     dropped)

     Routes: /login, /register, / (repo list + "New repository"), /:owner/:repo
     (file tree + branch switcher + latest commit bar),
     /:owner/:repo/blob/:ref/* (viewer, "Edit" button for owner),
     /:owner/:repo/edit/:ref/* (editor + commit message form),
     /:owner/:repo/commits/:ref (history), /:owner/:repo/commit/:oid (diff
     view).
     Every page has loading / empty / error states. Short oids shown as 7 chars;
     dates DD.MM.YYYY HH:mm.

     8. One-week schedule (≈14 tasks, backlog in tasks.md)

     ┌─────┬────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
     ┐
     │ day │                                goal
     │
     ├─────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
     ┤
     │ 1   │ Go + React skeletons, schema on Neon, auth (register/login/JWT)
     │
     ├─────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
     ┤
     │ 2   │ Object store: hashing, blob/tree/commit serialize + parse, with
     │
     │     │ golden-vector tests (pure Go, no DB)
     │
     ├─────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
     ┤
     │ 3   │ Repo endpoints; commit-writing endpoint (tree rebuild + ref CAS)
     │
     ├─────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
     ┤
     │ 4   │ Read endpoints: tree, blob, commit log, branches
     │
     ├─────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
     ┤
     │ 5   │ Commit detail + diff; frontend shell, auth pages, repo list
     │
     ├─────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
     ┤
     │ 6   │ Repo page, file viewer, history, diff view, editor/commit form
     │
     ├─────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
     ┤
     │ 7   │ Stretch (ff-merge) only if all green; polish, README, demo script,
     │
     │     │  final QA
     │
     └─────┴────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
     ┘

     9. Open risks

     - Tree rebuild is the hardest piece (nested paths, deletes, empty dirs):
       isolate it in a pure function tested without the DB.
     - Process overhead: 3 subagents × ~14 tasks is heavy for a week (see team
       suggestions below).

     Team folder suggestions (to report to the user, not implemented without 
     approval)

     Observations:
     - process.md still points to L0nelyG0d/BudgetControl; tasks.md and
       design-system.md (tenge, budget status, pie charts) are for the old
       project; AGENTS.md describes plan.md as having "money rules".
     - task_template.md typos: "addiction" → "addition", "consulate" →
       "consult".
     - Roles don't tell the engineer to read testing-guidelines.md /
       design-system.md; QA has no instruction to run the UI.

     Suggestions:
     1. Don't add a 4th role. Three is already heavy for one week. If anything,
        add a light Tech Lead / Reviewer responsibility only for days 2–3
        (object store + tree rebuild), because that is where subtle bugs live;
        otherwise fold it into QA ("also check against plan.md rules").
     2. Groom the whole backlog in one PM pass (day 1) instead of one subagent
        per task per step 2; per-task grooming only for tasks that change. Saves
        ~14 launches.
     3. Merge small tasks / allow QA batching for pure-frontend pages: QA
        verifies 2–3 related UI tasks together, still PASS/FAIL per issue.
     4. Modify constraints: engineer must read testing-guidelines.md (and
        design-system.md for UI); task constraint "hash functions must be
        verified against git test vectors"; keep "ask me if unsure" but add
        "otherwise pick the simplest option and note it in the issue comment" so
        it doesn't block.
     5. QA: add "for UI tasks, start the app and check the screen" and "check
        auth cases (401/403) from testing-guidelines".
     6. Replace old-project content: rewrite tasks.md, trim design-system.md
        money/budget/chart sections, fix repo name in process.md.

⏺ I filled out _docs/plan.md for micro git. I only wrote that file and haven't
  touched any other doc. I checked the two hash examples in it with real git 
  hash-object, and both match.

  What the plan says

  - Core idea: the backend stores blob, tree and commit objects in git's exact
    format. The commit ids come out the same as real git's, which is a good
    thing to show in your demo.
  - Features:
    - Register and log in.
    - Create repos; anyone can read them, only the owner can write.
    - Commit file changes from the browser.
    - Browse the file tree, history, branches and per-commit diffs.
  - Left out: clone, push, merges, pull requests and private repos.
  - Tables: users, repos, objects and refs.
  - Rest of the file:
    - The API endpoints.
    - Validation rules: path, size and message limits, and the 401/403/409
      cases.
    - The frontend routes.
    - A 7-day schedule. The only stretch goal is a fast-forward merge on day 7,
      and only if everything else is green.
  - Hardest part: rebuilding the folder structure when files are added or
    deleted. I suggest writing it as a pure function and testing it without the
    database.

  Team folder suggestions

  1. Don't add a 4th role. Three roles per task is already a lot for one week.
     If you want extra review, have QA also check the work against plan.md on
     days 2–3, when the object store and commit logic get built.
  2. Groom the whole backlog in one PM pass on day 1. Right now every task
     launches a separate PM subagent, which adds about 14 launches.
  3. Let QA check 2–3 related frontend tasks together. Each issue still gets its
     own PASS or FAIL.
  4. Change the constraints:
     - The engineer must read testing-guidelines.md, and design-system.md for UI
       work.
     - Hashing code must be checked against the git test values.
     - Add "if unsure, pick the simplest option and note it in the issue" so
       nothing stalls waiting for you.
  5. Tell QA to run the app for UI tasks. QA should also check the 401 and 403
     cases from the testing guidelines.

  - AGENTS.md says plan.md has "money rules".
  - task_template.md has typos: "addiction" should be "addition" and "consulate"
    should be "consult".

      133 -Description: Follow-up to #25. A migration (up or down) is non
          --transactional when its first line is `-- migrate:no-transacti
          -on` (name and checksum rules unchanged, `0001` cannot use it, 
          -exactly one statement per file). Pending migrations still run 
          -in version order; consecutive transactional ones share one tra
          -nsaction and a non-transactional one commits the open group fi
          -rst, so atomicity is per group. A non-transactional migration 
          -writes a row to `schema_migrations_in_progress`, runs its stat
          -ement outside a transaction, then records it in `schema_migrat
          -ions` and removes the row in its own transaction. When the mig
          -ration set contains a non-transactional file the advisory lock
          - is a session-level lock, taken before any transaction (waiter
          -s poll, so `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY` cannot deadlock with th
          -em) and held for the whole run. Any failure leaves the in-prog
          -ress row; startup, `migrate up` and `migrate down` then fail w
          -ith `did not finish` and the recovery SQL, and `migrate status
          -` shows the migration as `pending` / `interrupted` (exit 1). A
          - non-transactional down file is marked the same way. Go tests

  **plan.md
  (/Users/akniyetislambekkyzy/Desktop/Assignnment2-webfront/_docs/plan.md)**
  - New "Clarifications" block under Rules.
    - Usernames are lower-cased on register and login, and `api` is reserved.
    - Password is 8-72 bytes.
    - Ref resolution is branch name first, then a full 40-hex oid.
    - Branch names can't be `.`, `..` or look like an oid.
    - Author line format is defined.
    - `changes` semantics are defined, including duplicate paths, content plus
  delete, and directory delete.
    - `expected_tip` rules, order of checks (401, 404, 403, 400, 409), and diff
  edge cases (3 context lines, 5000-line truncation) are set.
    - The first commit on an empty repo must target the default branch.
    - Request bodies over 64 MiB get 413.
  - New "API contracts" section covering all 17 endpoints.
    - It has request JSON, response JSON, status codes and exact error strings.
    - It includes the Repo, Branch, tree, blob, commit-list, commit-detail and
  diff-hunk shapes.
    - Lists are wrapped in objects.
    - The `409` response carries `current_tip`.
  - Frontend: added a `/:owner/:repo/new/:ref` route, plus owner-only and
  branch-only rules for Edit, Add file and Delete file.

  **Issue comments**
  - #3: column types and unique indexes assumed.

  - #9: repo contract and check order.

  it's a stack decision.

› Message from general-purpose
  Issue #6 is implemented in
  /Users/akniyetislambekkyzy/Desktop/Assignnment2-webfront/backend/gitobj, in
  one commit (9804e8a, `Closes #6`). It uses only the standard library. `go test
  ./...` and `go test -race ./...` both pass in backend/. I commented on the
  issue and left it open for QA. All criteria were implemented unchanged. I did
  not add a separate blob parse/round-trip function, because a blob body is just
  its content.

  Fixtures come from real git 2.39.3, regenerated with `testdata/gen.sh`. They
  cover tree ordering (`foo.txt` before `foo/`), nested directories, and commits
  with zero and one parent.

  Exported API:
  - `Type` with `TypeBlob`, `TypeTree`, `TypeCommit`; constants `ModeFile`
  ("100644"), `ModeDir` ("40000"), `OIDLen`
  - `Hash(Type, body) string`, `HashBlob(content) string`, `ValidOID(string)
  bool`
  - `TreeEntry{Mode, Name, OID}` with `IsDir()`
  - `SortTreeEntries([]TreeEntry)`
  - `SerializeTree([]TreeEntry) ([]byte, error)`: sorts git-style and validates;
  the input slice is not modified
  - `ParseTree([]byte) ([]TreeEntry, error)`
  - `Signature{Name, Email, Unix, TZ}` (an empty `TZ` means "+0000")
  - `Commit{Tree, Parents, Author, Committer, Message}`
  - `SerializeCommit(Commit) ([]byte, error)`: trims the message and adds one
  trailing `\n`
  - `ParseCommit([]byte) (Commit, error)`: the parsed `Message` keeps its
  trailing newline

✻ Cogitated for 21s · done Thursday 5:24 PM

› Message from general-purpose
  Issue #7 is implemented in `backend/treebuild` and committed on main as
  6e1bfcf. The commit message has `Closes #7`, and nothing is pushed. `go test
  ./...` and `go test -race ./...` pass. I commented on the issue and left it
  open. `gitobj` is untouched and `_docs/questions.md` is unchanged.

  Exported API:
  - **Store interface:** `Store` with `GetObject(oid) (gitobj.Type, []byte,
  error)` and `PutObject(t, body) (oid, error)`.
  - **In-memory store:** `ErrNotFound`, `MemStore`, `NewMemStore()` (`Len`,
  `Has`).
  - **Changes:** `Change{Path, Content *string, Delete}`, `Upsert(path,
  content)`, `Remove(path)`.
  - **Entry points:** `Apply(store, rootOID, changes) (newRootOID, error)` and
  `ValidatePath`. An empty `rootOID` means the empty tree.
  - **Errors:** `*Error{Kind, Path}`, with sentinels `ErrInvalidPath`,
  `ErrDuplicatePath`, `ErrBadChange`, `ErrFileTooLarge`, `ErrNotUTF8Text`,
  `ErrPathConflict`, `ErrNoChanges`. `Error()` renders the plan.md message,
  "<kind>: <path>".

  Two choices of mine, both noted on the issue:
  - Deleting a directory path returns `ErrInvalidPath`, because plan.md has no
  dedicated message for it.
  - A delete whose path is a prefix of, or lies under, another change in the
  same call counts as a path conflict.

  root package tests. A comment is posted on the issue, which is still open. I
  did not push, and `_docs/questions.md` is untouched.

  Exported API:
  - `Diff(old, new string) Result`, where `Result{Hunks, Additions, Deletions,
  Truncated}`
  - `Trees(store treebuild.Store, oldTree, newTree string) ([]FileDiff, error)`,
  sorted by path. `""` or `EmptyTreeOID` means the empty tree.
  - Types `FileDiff{Path, Status, Additions, Deletions, Truncated, Hunks}`,
  `Hunk` and `Line`, with snake_case JSON tags.
  - `SplitLines`, plus the constants `ContextLines`, `MaxLines`,
  `Context`/`Add`/`Del` and `StatusAdded`/`StatusModified`/`StatusDeleted`.

  Hunk headers and bodies match `git diff -U3` on the test fixtures. Two choices
  of mine: a truncated file reports additions and deletions as 0, and a
  file/directory swap shows as the file deleted plus the new files added.

  - **Text diff vs git:** I ran `git diff -U3 --minimal --no-index` against 25
  fixtures in a scratchpad copy: inserts, deletes, moved blocks, CRLF, empty
  files, blank lines, duplicate lines, and context gaps of 5, 6 and 7 lines.
  Hunk headers and lines match git everywhere except the two
  missing-final-newline fixtures. Those differ because the issue says a missing
  final newline is not a difference.
  - **Random inputs:** 400 random pairs gave add/del counts equal to git in
  every one. In 48 the hunk layout differs, only where several equally short
  diffs exist. A 3000-case check that re-applies the hunks to the old text
  rebuilds the new text every time.
  - **5000-line cap:** 5000 lines per side is diffed. 5001 lines on either side,
  with or without a final newline, gives `hunks: []` and `truncated: true`.
  - **Tree diff:** I built real git trees from two commits, loaded them into an
  in-memory store, and compared against `git diff-tree`. File statuses match,
  paths are sorted, and unchanged subtrees and blobs are never read. An empty or
  nil old tree (root commit) gives every file `added`. A file swapped for a
  directory (and the reverse) shows as deleted plus added, and a rename shows as
  delete plus add.

  Note: truncated files report additions and deletions of 0, which the
  implementation documents.

› Message from general-purpose
  Issue #5 is implemented and committed on main as d55f369 ("Closes #5"), as one
  commit. Not pushed, issue still open, and I posted the comment on it. `go
  test ./...` and `go test -race ./...` pass with TEST_DATABASE_URL set. No
  questions.md entry was needed.

  Login, logout and /auth/me are in `backend/session.go`. Config requires
  JWT_SECRET and reads COOKIE_SECURE. Tokens are HS256 only, with `exp` required
  and the clock taken from `Server.Now`.

  Middleware API for #9-#12 (all in `session.go`):
  - `s.requireAuth(h http.HandlerFunc) http.HandlerFunc`: returns 401
  "authentication required" without running `h`. It also returns 401 when the
  token's user no longer exists, because it checks the DB.
  - `s.optionalAuth(h)`: always runs `h`, with the user set only if logged in.
  - `UserFromContext(ctx) (User, bool)`, where `User{ID int64, Username
  string}`.
  - Fields `Server.JWTSecret` and `Server.CookieSecure`. `NewServer(db)` is
  unchanged, so tests set the secret themselves. `testServer` in `auth_test.go`
  already does.

  Test note: the full suite takes about 60s, and the race run about 80s.

  - **Register and login:** register works. Login returns 200 `{id, username}`
  with `Path=/; Max-Age=604800; HttpOnly; SameSite=Lax`, and no `Secure` flag by
  default. The token is not in the body.
  - **Secure flag:** `Secure` appears only when `COOKIE_SECURE=true`.
  - **Failed logins:** wrong password and unknown user give byte-identical 401
  `invalid username or password` (compared without the Date header).
  - **`/auth/me`:** a valid cookie returns 200. A missing cookie, garbage token,
  expired token (forged with my own secret), token signed with a different
  secret, `alg=none` and an empty cookie all return 401.
  - **Logout:** idempotent 204 that clears the cookie, with or without a cookie.
  - **Deleted user:** the token of a deleted user returns 401.
  - **No `JWT_SECRET`:** the server exits with `config: JWT_SECRET is not set`.
  - **Cleanup:** the test users were deleted and 0 `qa-i5-%` rows remain.

  One thing I noticed, not a criterion: register rejects usernames with
  underscores. The rule is 3-32 characters from a-z, 0-9 and `-`.

› Message from general-purpose
  Issue #9 is implemented and committed on main (d3d8a9b, then 4f3b211 with
  `Closes #9`). `go test ./...` and `go test -race ./...` pass against the Neon

  FK).

  API for #10-#12 (package main, `backend/store.go`, `backend/repos.go`):
  - `NewRepoStore(db, repoID) *RepoStore`. `db` can be the pool or a pgx.Tx.
  - `rs.Objects(ctx)` returns a `treebuild.Store` (insert-if-absent,
  `treebuild.ErrNotFound`).
  - Refs: `rs.GetRef`, `rs.ListRefs`, `rs.CreateRef` (`ErrRefExists`),
  `rs.DeleteRef` (`ErrRefNotFound`).
  - `rs.UpdateRef(ctx, name, oldOID, newOID)` is the compare-and-swap. An empty
  `oldOID` means the ref must not exist. A lost swap returns `ErrRefMoved`.
  - `rs.ResolveCommit(ctx, ref)` takes a branch name or a full 40-char oid and
  returns the commit oid or `ErrRefNotFound`.
  - Handler helpers: `s.findRepo`, `s.repoFromPath` (404), `s.ownedRepoFromPath`
  (404 then 403). Wrap write handlers in `s.requireAuth` so 401 comes first.
  - `methods{}` in `handler.go` lets one path serve several HTTP methods with a
  JSON 405.

  Test helpers in `repos_test.go`: `newUserCookie`, `createRepo`. In
  `store_test.go`: `newRepoStore`.

  and handler.go are identical to that commit in the local repo.
  - **Commit tests:** `go test -p 1 -count=1 -timeout 15m -run Commit ./`
  against the Neon test branch passed all 9 tests. The same subset with `-race`
  also passed.
  - **Static and no-DB runs:** `go vet ./...` is clean. The full suite without
  TEST_DATABASE_URL passed in all 5 packages, with the DB tests skipped.
  - **Real server with curl:**
    - 201 on the first commit to an empty repo, with `parent` null.
    - Nested files, edit, delete (the empty directory disappears) and a
  multi-file commit all work.
    - The 400 cases return the plan.md messages: no changes, duplicate path,
  content plus delete, NUL, invalid paths, directory delete, file/directory
  conflicts, 0 or 51 changes, bad message length, bad tip, bad branch name and a
  file over 1 MiB.
    - A stale tip gives 409 with `current_tip`.
    - A 70 MB body gives 413.
    - The check order 401, 404, 403, 400 holds.
    - Two parallel requests with the same tip gave one 201 and one 409, for both
  the first-commit and existing-branch cases.
    - A 1 MiB file with a 500-character message, and 50 changes, are accepted.
  - **Real git on the stored objects:** I rebuilt the stored objects into
  scratch git repos. All hashes match their oids. `git fsck --strict --full` is
  clean on all 3 repos, and `git log --stat` and `git ls-tree` match the
  expected history.
  - **Cleanup:** all qa10-* users and their repos are deleted from the test
  branch.

  **Observations, not failures:** a lone surrogate or invalid UTF-8 in the JSON
  body is turned into U+FFFD by Go's decoder and stored, rather than rejected
  with 400. NUL is the only way to reach the "not valid UTF-8" 400 over HTTP.
  The branch name `a..b` is accepted, which matches the plan's `[A-Za-z0-9._-]+`
  rule.

✻ Worked for 11s · done Friday 11:04 AM

› Message from general-purpose
  QA verdict for issue #11 at commit e3b91f0: FAIL. One acceptance criterion
  fails, and the cause is the net/http ServeMux redirect, not the handler code.
  I posted the comment, which starts `## QA: FAIL`:
  https://github.com/L0nelyG0d/micro-git/issues/11#issuecomment-6209321831. The
  issue is not closed and no repo code was changed.

  **Failing criterion:** "Paths violating the path rules (`..`, empty segment,
  `.git` segment, over 255 characters) are `400 invalid path`".
  - Literal `..`, `.` and `//` segments never reach the handler. With `curl
  --path-as-is`, `tree/main/../src`, `tree/main/./src`, `tree/main//src`,
  `blob/main//README.md` and `tree/main/src/..` all return `307` to the cleaned
  path.
  - A `.git` segment, a 256-character segment and percent-encoded `%2e%2e` /
  `%2F..%2F` all return `400 invalid path` correctly.
  - I marked it FAIL, and the comment names the cause (plan.md contract versus
  the stdlib routing constraint in AGENTS.md).
  - A system analyst needs to decide between two options:
    - Relax the criterion to "redirected or not served".
    - Require a wrapper that runs before the mux.
  - `TestReadingUncleanPathRedirects` asserts the 307, so the test suite already
  encodes the stdlib behaviour.

  **Passing criteria:** all other criteria pass, each checked with curl against
  the real server.
  - **Branches:** list, create (201 with `oid` equal to the source tip), 409,
  and 404 for a missing `from` (including on an empty repo). Invalid names
  return 400, including 101 characters and a 40-hex name, while a 100-character
  name is accepted. Delete returns 204, the default branch returns 400, and a
  missing branch returns 404.
  - **Branch delete and write order:** objects are kept after a branch delete.
  The 401, 404, 403, 400 order holds on create and delete.
  - **Tree and blob:**
    - A ref resolves as a branch name first, then a full commit oid. An
  abbreviated oid, a tree oid, a blob oid, a cross-repo oid and an unknown name
  all give `404 ref not found`.
    - Root, trailing-slash and nested paths work, and `%20` is decoded.
    - A file or directory used the wrong way gives 400, and a missing path gives
  404.
    - `size` is in bytes (10 for `héllo€\n`), and an empty file gives `size:0`.
    - An empty repo gives `404 ref not found`, and reading at a commit oid
  returns that commit's tree.
    - Anonymous reads work, and the `commit` field equals the branch tip.

  **Tests run** (from `backend/` in a clean copy under the scratchpad, with `-p
  1`):
  - `go test -run 'Branch|Tree|Blob|Read' ./...` with TEST_DATABASE_URL: all
  pass.
  - `go vet ./...`: clean.
  - The same subset with `-race`: pass.
  - The whole suite without TEST_DATABASE_URL (database tests skip): pass.

  **Cleanup:** I deleted every repo I created and both test users (`qaa`,
  `qab`). A final query showed 0 rows left, and the server is stopped.

  before any handler runs. The system analyst relaxed the contract in plan.md
  and issue #11 to match.

⏺ Background command "cat >> backend/db/schema.sql <<'EOF'

-- A ref can only point at an object that exists in the same repo.
DO $$
BEGIN
    IF NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM pg_constraint WHERE conname = 'refs_object_fk')
THEN
        ALTER TABLE refs ADD CONSTRAINT refs_object_fk
            FOREIGN KEY (repo_id, oid) REFERENCES objects (repo_id, oid);
    END IF;
END $$;
EOF
python3 - <<'EOF'
p='_docs/plan.md'
s=open(p).read()
s=s.replace("| `refs` | `repo_id` → repos (on delete cascade), `name`, `oid`;
primary key `(repo_id, name)` |","| `refs` | `repo_id` → repos (on delete
cascade), `name`, `oid`; primary key `(repo_id, name)`; foreign key `(repo_id,
oid)` → objects, so a branch can only point at a stored object (write the commit
object before moving the ref) |")
open(p,'w').write(s)
EOF
for v in DATABASE_URL TEST_DATABASE_URL; do export $v="$(grep "^$v=" .env | cut
-d= -f2-)"; done
for u in "$TEST_DATABASE_URL" "$DATABASE_URL"; do for i in 1 2 3; do
PGCONNECT_TIMEOUT=20 psql "$u" -q -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -f backend/db/schema.sql &&
echo applied && break; done; done
export JWT_SECRET="$(grep '^JWT_SECRET=' .env | cut -d= -f2-)"
cd backend && go test -p 1 -race ./... 2>&1 | tail -15" completed (exit code 0)

› Message from general-purpose
  Issue #23 is done on branch `issue-23-refs-fk`. The head commit is 9af669a,
  rebased onto main, not pushed, and the issue is still open. `go test -p 1
  -race ./...` passes for all four backend packages, with the root package
  taking 696s. I commented on the issue:
  https://github.com/L0nelyG0d/micro-git/issues/23#issuecomment-6211883888

  - **Production code:** no change was needed. `createCommit` already writes the
  commit object in the same transaction before `UpdateRef`, and `createBranch`
  only copies the tip of an existing ref.
  - **Existing tests fixed:** `TestRefStore`, `TestResolveCommit`,
  `TestEmptyFlag` and `TestDeleteRepo` were pointing refs at made-up oids. They
  now store real objects first, and `TestResolveCommit` also stores the blob in
  the second repo. `TestDeleteRepo` still checks that deleting a repo cascades.
  - **New test:** `TestRefNeedsObject` in `backend/store_test.go`. Create, first
  update and swap to a missing oid, and create with an oid that only exists in
  another repo, all fail with Postgres error 23503. The existing refs are
  unchanged afterwards.
  - **Timeout cause:** not the FK, not a deadlock and not a cold start. The
  database was warm on the first try.
    - **Latency:** each statement to Neon takes about 200ms. 100 `select 1` took
  20s, and so did 100 object inserts, so the FK adds nothing measurable.
    - **Test length:** the root package has long serial tests. `TestCommitLog`
  alone takes about 210s under `-race`, and the whole package needs about 700s.
    - **Timeouts:** a 5m `-timeout` fails it (301s), and the earlier 600s limit
  was also too short. I ran it with `-timeout 30m`.
    - **Possible follow-up:** batch the serial inserts in the history test
  fixture (`newHistFixture` in `history_test.go`).

  QA: PASS for issue #23, branch issue-23-refs-fk, commit 9af669a (detached
  checkout). The verdict is posted:
  https://github.com/L0nelyG0d/micro-git/issues/23#issuecomment-6212101888

  - **FK on the test DB:** `\d refs` shows `refs_object_fk` on `(repo_id, oid)`
  referencing `objects`. The original cascade FK to `repos` is still there.
  - **Schema idempotent:** I applied `backend/db/schema.sql` twice with
  ON_ERROR_STOP. Both runs succeeded with only "already exists" notices, and
  `pg_constraint` has exactly one `refs_object_fk`.
  - **plan.md matches:** the `refs` row (line 42) documents the composite FK and
  the rule to write the object before moving the ref.
  - **Write order:** only `CreateRef` and `UpdateRef` touch refs. The commit
  path puts the commit object and then updates the ref in one transaction.
  Branch creation copies an existing tip.
  - **TestRefNeedsObject:** it has four cases, and each must fail with Postgres
  error 23503, which is the FK violation:
    - create with a missing oid
    - create with an oid that exists only in another repo
    - first update with a missing oid
    - swap to a missing oid

    It then checks the refs are unchanged. Because the error is 23503 and not
  the "ref moved" or "ref exists" errors, the FK is what rejects the write.
  - **Cascade:** `TestDeleteRepo` still checks that deleting a repo cascades.
  - **Tests:**
    - `go vet ./...` is clean.
    - `go test -p 1 -race -timeout 30m ./...` passed all 4 packages with 0
  failures. The root package took 716s; the other three took about 1 to 2s each.
  - **Timeout cause:** the earlier 600s timeout is the ~200ms Neon round trip
  per statement, not the FK or a deadlock. Anyone running the full suite needs
  `-timeout 30m`.

  No code was changed, merged or pushed. I wrote helper scripts only in the
  scratchpad.

✻ Brewed for 27s · done Friday 2:10 PM

› Message from general-purpose
  #21 is split into four groomed issues, commented on and closed. I wrote no
  code. The contracts are committed on main and not pushed.

  **New issues** (each has backend and frontend as separate acceptance criteria,
  except #26, which is frontend only):
  - **#24 Fast-forward merge:** `POST /repos/{owner}/{repo}/merge` with `{base,
  head}`.
    - It is owner only and moves `base` with the existing ref compare-and-swap.
    - It returns 409 `not a fast-forward` or 409 `branch tip has moved`.
    - It returns 400 for already up to date and for merging a branch into
  itself.
    - The frontend is a Merge dialog on the repo page.
  - **#25 Compare:** `GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/compare?base=&head=`.
    - It diffs the two tip trees and reuses the commit-detail `files` shape and
  diff code.
    - The frontend is a `/:owner/:repo/compare?base=&head=` page that reuses the
  existing diff component.
  - **#26 README:** frontend only, with no API change.
    - It reads `README.md` at the repo root through the existing blob endpoint
  and shows it in a card under the file tree.
    - A small in-repo renderer outputs React elements only: no
  `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`, no new packages.
    - The supported subset is headings, paragraphs, lists, fenced code, inline
  code, bold and italic. Links, images, tables and HTML show as plain text.
  - **#27 Rename:** `PATCH /repos/{owner}/{repo}` with `{name}`.
    - It is owner only. It returns 400 `invalid repository name` and 409
  `repository already exists`.
    - A case-only change or the same name returns 200.
    - The old name returns 404 afterwards.
    - The frontend is a Rename dialog that navigates to the new URL.

  **Repo changes**
  - `_docs/plan.md` has a new "Stretch endpoints" subsection under API
  contracts, just before `## Frontend`.
  - I also added a line to `_docs/tasks.md` pointing #21 at #24-#27, which you
  didn't ask for.
  - Commit `63c4d63` is "Add stretch API contracts, Refs #21", with the
  Co-Authored-By trailer.

  **Design questions** (I took the simplest default for each and wrote it into
  the issues, so nobody is blocked):
  1. **Rename redirects:** Should the old repo name redirect to the new one?
  Recommendation: no redirects. The old name returns 404, which needs no extra
  table.
  2. **Merge when already up to date:** Should "already up to date" be a 400
  error or a 200 no-op? Recommendation: 400 `already up to date`.
  3. **Compare route shape:** Should compare use query parameters or `a...b` in
  the path? Recommendation: query parameters, because branch names may contain
  dots, so a three-dot path would be ambiguous.
  4. **README rendering:** Do you want a real markdown renderer with links and
  images? That would need a package, and none is approved in `design-system.md`.
  Recommendation: keep the safe in-repo subset (#26) and decide on links later.

  None of these is blocking. I didn't append them to `_docs/questions.md`
  because you asked for them in the report.

› Message from general-purpose
  Issue #25 (compare two branches) is implemented on branch `issue-25-compare`,
  head `19ee841`. It is rebased on current main (includes the #26 merge), with
  two commits that reference #25. Nothing is pushed or merged, and the issue is
  still open with a hand-over comment.

  **Backend**
  - `GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/compare` is in `backend/compare.go`, with a
  one-block route added in `handler.go`.
  - It reuses `resolveRef`, `loadCommit` and `linediff.Trees`, so there is no
  second diff implementation and the commit endpoint is unchanged.
  - It returns `400` `base and head are required`, `404` `repository not found`
  or `ref not found`, and `200` with `files: []` for identical tips. No auth is
  needed.
  - `backend/compare_test.go` covers added, modified and deleted files, a
  reversed compare (two tips, not merge base), oid refs, identical tips and
  parity with the commit detail. The error cases are missing and empty params,
  an unknown base or head, an unknown oid, an empty repo and an unknown repo.

  **Frontend**
  - The diff component (`FileView`) now lives in
  `frontend/src/components/DiffView.tsx`, extracted from `CommitPage.tsx`; its
  rendering is unchanged.
  - `frontend/src/pages/ComparePage.tsx` is the new page at
  `/:owner/:repo/compare?base=&head=`. It has "Base" and "Compare" selects
  prefilled from the query string, and changing one updates the URL and reloads.
  It shows a summary line, the per-file diff, the "No differences between these
  branches" empty state, `Skeleton` loading, an error with retry, and the
  empty-repo hint.
  - Shared files touched minimally: one route line in `App.tsx`, and in
  `RepoPage.tsx` a "Compare" button added beside "Add file" inside a new wrapper
  `div` (a small localized change to a shared file).
  - `ComparePage.test.tsx` has 6 tests: diff rendering with truncated note,
  identical tips, changing a select, error and retry, the empty repo, and the
  repo page link.

  **Test results**
  - `npm test`: 108 passed. `npm run build`: ok.
  - Backend `go test -p 1 -race -timeout 30m ./...`: everything passed except 6
  tests (TestMeExpiresWithClock, TestRequireAndOptionalAuth, TestObjectStore,
  TestRefStore, TestResolveCommit, TestRefNeedsObject). Those failed because
  Neon DNS dropped late in the run ("no such host"). I re-ran those 6 plus
  TestCompare alone with `-race` and they passed.
  - The `gitobj`, `linediff` and `treebuild` packages passed in the full run.

  Missing `base` or `head` query values on the page fall back to the default
  branch, which shows "No differences". I noted this in a comment in
  `ComparePage.tsx`.

  Another agent overwrote the shared scratchpad `gotest.sh` mid-session, so I
  used `se25-gotest.sh` for my runs.

  **Backend**
  - `PATCH /repos/{owner}/{repo}` is in the new `backend/rename.go`, with one
  route line added in `backend/handler.go`.
  - Checks run in the order 401, 404, 403, 400, 409. A missing, null, wrongly
  typed or unknown-field body is 400 `invalid JSON body`.
  - A unique-violation from the database maps to 409. A same-name or case-only
  rename returns 200.
  - Tests are in `backend/rename_test.go` and cover every case the issue lists.
  - I also changed the Allow-header assertion in `backend/repos_test.go` to
  `DELETE, GET, PATCH`, because the new route changes it.

  labelled input prefilled with the current name, client-side validation, and
  submit disabled while the request runs.
  - A 409 shows the duplicate-name message inline; a 400 shows the name rule.
  - On success it shows a toast and navigates to the new name, replacing the
  history entry.
  - `frontend/src/pages/RepoPage.tsx` has a minimal edit: an import, and a
  Rename button next to Delete for the owner only.
  - Tests are in `frontend/src/pages/RenameRepo.test.tsx`: success with
  navigation, 409, 400, non-owner button hidden, and the old URL's not-found

› Message from general-purpose
  Issue #24 (fast-forward merge) is implemented on branch `issue-24-ff-merge`,
  head `3a98fb9`. The branch is rebased on current main (includes #26), not
  pushed, and the issue is still open. All the acceptance criteria I implemented
  are covered by tests.

  Tests:
  - **Backend:** `go test -p 1 -race ./...` is green. The first full run failed
  because the Neon connection dropped partway through (DNS errors, and one
  username collision on the shared test database); the rerun passed.
  - **Frontend:** `npm test` shows 110 passing and `npm run build` succeeds, run
  after the rebase.

  I commented on the issue with the branch, head commit and results.

  Files:
  - **Backend:**
    - `backend/merge.go` holds the handler.
    - `backend/merge_test.go` has the tests.
    - `backend/handler.go` gets one route registration.
  - **Frontend:**
    - `frontend/src/components/MergeDialog.tsx` is the new dialog.
    - `frontend/src/pages/MergeDialog.test.tsx` has the tests.
    - `frontend/src/pages/RepoPage.tsx` gets one import and one JSX block. That
  block sits right after the `BranchSwitcher` ternary, so it may conflict with
  changes from #25 or #27 in the same area.

  Choices I made:
  - **Concurrency test:** the concurrent tip move is tested through an
  unexported package variable `beforeMergeSwap` in `merge.go`. It is nil in
  production and lets the test move the base tip just before the
  compare-and-swap.
  - **Ancestor walk:** it visits each commit once and stops after as many
  commits as the repository has.
  - **Dialog defaults:** "Into" starts as the default branch. "Merge from"
  starts as the branch currently shown, if that isn't the target, otherwise the
  first other branch.
  - **"Reload" action:** it closes the dialog and reloads the repo, branch list,
  tree and commit data.
  - **After a successful merge:** the page navigates to the target branch if you
  weren't on it. Otherwise it reloads the tree and latest-commit bar.

✻ Worked for 17s · done Friday 8:17 PM

› Message from general-purpose
  I filed 8 issues (#29 to #36) and committed `_docs/tasks.md` on main as
  d1351e6, "Add next-round backlog, Refs #29". It has a new "Next round (issues
  #29-#36)" section with the conflict map. I did not push, and I changed no
  code. I did not run the backend or frontend test suites, so the 12-minute
  suite claim is taken from the README and the fixture code. `gofmt -l`, `go vet
  ./...` and `tsc -b` came back clean.

  When I started, #28 was the only open issue and nobody had filed bugs from it.
  Each issue follows the template, with the files touched and the conflicts
  listed under Constraints.

  ## Issues
  - **#29 Backend: commit log in one query instead of one per commit.**
    - The log does one query per commit: 51 for page 1, 50*N+1 for page N.
    - The repo page calls page 1 on every view just to show the latest-commit
  bar.
    - The fix is a recursive CTE.
    - It carries a guard test for a message that contains a `parent <oid>` line.
  - **#30 Backend tests: shared pool, cheap bcrypt, batched fixtures.**
    - Every `testServer` call opens and pings a new pool.
    - Every test user costs a register and a login at bcrypt default cost.
    - `newHistFixture` does about 240 serial round trips.
    - The target is at least 40% faster.
  - **#31 Backend: server timeouts, graceful shutdown, secret length, safe
  headers.**
    - `http.ListenAndServe` has no timeouts, so slow clients can hold
  connections.
    - It also adds a minimum `JWT_SECRET` length of 32 bytes, `nosniff`, and
  `no-store` on `/auth/*`.
  - **#32 Backend: cap JSON body size and total diff work.**
    - `decodeJSON` has no body limit on register, login, branches, merge or
  rename.
    - A commit with 50 files of 5000 lines can force about 1.25 billion LCS cell
  operations on an unauthenticated GET.
    - The issue adds a 1 MiB body limit and a 32M-cell diff budget, with
  `plan.md` updated.
  - **#33 Backend: concurrency tests and 404 for repo deleted mid-commit.**
    - It adds concurrency tests for commit, first commit, rename and branch
  create.
    - A repo deleted while a commit is in flight currently returns 500
  (foreign-key violation). The issue maps that to 404.
  - **#34 Frontend: session-expired feedback and 401 tests for owner actions.**
    - There is no 401 test for delete-branch, create-branch, merge, rename or
  delete-repo.
    - After a 401 the owner buttons silently disappear.
    - The issue adds a toast in `expireSession`.
  - **#35 Frontend: page titles, skip link, focus, reduced motion, 320px
  layout.**
    - There is no `document.title` per route, no skip link, and no focus move on
  navigation.
    - There is no `prefers-reduced-motion` handling, although the design system
  requires it.
    - `BranchSwitcher` does not wrap on narrow screens.
  - **#36 Docs: sync plan and README with the code, remove stale files.**
    - `plan.md` lacks the `/tree/:ref/*` and `/compare` routes.
    - The README package layout omits `merge.go`, `compare.go` and `rename.go`.
    - Some comments still say "later tasks".
    - Four duplicate fixture files exist at
  `backend/gitobj/{commit0.bin,commit1.bin,tree.bin,oids.txt}`.

  ## Conflict map
  Issues that share a file must run in order.
  - **`backend/history_test.go`:** #29 and #30.
  - **`README.md`:** #30 (test duration text), #31 (env table row) and #36. #36
  must run last.
  - **`_docs/plan.md`:** #32 and #36.
  - **`backend/store.go`, `handler.go`, `config.go`:** #29 and #31 before #36.
  - **`RepoPage.tsx` and `BranchSwitcher.tsx`:** only #35. Serialize with any
  #28 bug fix that touches them.
  - **Fully independent, can run in parallel:** #29, #31, #32, #33, #34 and #35.
  #30 is independent too, apart from its shared file with #29. #33 only reads
  the test helpers #30 changes, so it should rebase on #30 if #30 merges first.
  - **Suggested waves:**
    - Wave 1: #29, #31, #32, #33, #34, #35, with #30 after or before #29.
    - Wave 2: #36.

  ## Architectural questions (not filed; for `_docs/questions.md`)
  1. **Logout does not revoke the JWT.** A stolen token works for 7 days.
     - Revoking it needs a `token_version` column on users, or a sessions table.
     - Default: no. Document it as a known limit, which #36 does.
  2. **Rate limiting login and register.** Bcrypt makes a flood expensive, and
  there is no lockout.
     - Default: no. Add an in-memory limiter only if the demo is public.
  3. **Denormalizing parents** into a `commits` table or column to speed up
  history and `isAncestor` in merge.
     - Default: no. #29's CTE is enough.
  4. **Paginating `GET /repos` or adding `per_page` to the commit list.** Both
  change the contract.
     - Default: no.
  5. **Garbage collection of objects** after a branch or repo delete. Branch
  delete never removes objects.
     - Default: no. Document it.
  6. **Adding CI (GitHub Actions) for `go test` and `npm test`.** The repo has
  none, and the Go tests need a database secret.
     - Default: no, because secrets and infrastructure are involved.
  7. **Defaulting `COOKIE_SECURE` to true**, or refusing to start without TLS.
     - Default: no. The project is local-only.
  8. **Real merges, tags, and a `ref` resolver accepting abbreviated oids.**
  These are already out of scope in `plan.md`.
     - Default: keep them out.

  ## Smaller ideas not filed (they would be follow-ups or could be added to #35)
  - **Editor guard:** an unsaved-changes guard (`beforeunload` plus an
  AlertDialog on Cancel). The client message length also uses UTF-16 units,
  while the server counts runes.
  - **`isAncestor` in merge:** it loads one commit per query, a follow-up once
  #29 proves out.
  - **Content-Type check:** require `application/json` on JSON bodies, as
  defense in depth.

  ## Heads-up for the controller
  #31 makes `JWT_SECRET` shorter than 32 bytes a startup error. The user's local
  `.env` may need a longer secret. The engineer is told to mention this in the
  PR comment and not to edit `.env`.

› Message from general-purpose
  Issue #35 is implemented and committed on `issue-35-a11y-titles` at `b177e11`,
  not pushed. The 320px and 1024px browser check is not done, because I had no
  browser. I commented on the issue asking QA to check it by hand.

  **What changed**
  - **Titles:** `TitleSync` in `App.tsx` (`useLocation` + `matchPath`) sets
  every title listed in the issue.
  - **Skip link and focus:** a skip link is the first tab stop and focuses
  `<main id="main" tabIndex={-1}>`. Focus also moves to `<main>` after a

✻ Churned for 10s · done Friday 8:33 PM

› Message from general-purpose
  Issue #31 is implemented and committed on `issue-31-server-hardening` (head
  b817753, one commit, `Refs #31`). It is not pushed, and main had not moved, so
  no rebase was needed. I commented on the issue
  (https://github.com/L0nelyG0d/micro-git/issues/31#issuecomment-6219050466).

  - **Server (`backend/main.go`):**
    - It builds an `http.Server` with named constants: 10s read-header, 90s
  read, 90s write, 120s idle, each with a one-line reason.
    - On SIGINT or SIGTERM it logs "shutting down", calls `Shutdown` with a 10s
  context, closes the pool and exits 0.
  - **Secret length (`backend/config.go`):** a `JWT_SECRET` under 32 bytes is
  rejected with `JWT_SECRET must be at least 32 bytes`. `backend/config_test.go`
  has cases for 31 bytes (error) and 32 bytes (ok), and the other cases now use
  a 32-byte secret. The README `JWT_SECRET` row says "At least 32 bytes".
  - **Headers (`backend/handler.go`):** a small `safeHeaders` function wraps the
  mux in `Handler()`. Every response gets `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`,
  and `/auth/*` also gets `Cache-Control: no-store`. `TestSafeHeaders` covers
  health, an unknown route, a 405, a bad-body login and `/auth/me` without a
  cookie.
  - **Manual shutdown check:** I ran the built server and sent SIGINT. It logged
  "shutting down" and exited with status 0 in about 1s. The 404 response
  carried `nosniff`.
  - **Tests:**
    - The affected tests pass.
    - `go test -p 1 -race -timeout 40m ./...` passes in all four packages (the
  root package took 895s).
    - `go vet` is clean and `gofmt` reports nothing.
    - I did not run the frontend tests; no frontend files changed.

  For the user: check that your local `.env` `JWT_SECRET` is at least 32 bytes,
  because the server now refuses to start otherwise. You said it is 64 chars,
  and I did not edit `.env`.

✻ Churned for 2s · done Friday 8:40 PM

› Message from general-purpose
  Issue #32 is implemented on branch `issue-32-body-diff-caps`, rebased on main;
  the issue comment is posted and the issue is left open. `go test -p 1 -race
  ./...` passes in every backend package. I didn't run the frontend tests, since
  no frontend files changed.

  Everything is in one commit, "Cap JSON bodies at 1 MiB and total diff work at
  32M cells" (Refs #32). Its short hash is on the issue comment:
  https://github.com/L0nelyG0d/micro-git/issues/32#issuecomment-6219066192

  - **Body limit:** `decodeJSON` and `decodeJSONQuiet` in `backend/httpx.go` now
  read through `http.MaxBytesReader` with a named 1 MiB limit. An oversized
  body gives `413` `{"error":"request too large"}`, including a garbage body
  over the limit. Commits keep their own 64 MiB limit.
  - **Quiet decoder callers:** `decodeJSONQuiet` now takes `w`, and the 413 is
  reported at the point where `invalid JSON body` would have been. I added a
  `badBody(w, r)` helper in `backend/httpx.go` and used it in
  `backend/branches.go`, `backend/merge.go` and `backend/rename.go`, one or two
  lines each. Those three files are outside the issue's file list; I needed them
  for the 413 to reach the quiet-decoder handlers.
  - **Diff budget:** `linediff.Trees` collects the changed files, sorts them by
  path, then diffs each against a shared `MaxTotalCells = 32_000_000` budget.
  The cost of a file is its prefix/suffix-trimmed old lines times new lines. A
  file that doesn't fit is reported as truncated, like an over-long file
  (`truncated: true`, `hunks: []`, zero counts). The change is in
  `backend/linediff/text.go` and `backend/linediff/tree.go`.
  - **Tests:** the new `backend/httpx_test.go` sends 1 MiB + 1 byte to
  `/auth/register` and `/auth/login` and gets `413`; a body just under the limit
  with valid JSON still decodes. `backend/linediff/linediff_test.go` now covers
  budget exhaustion, small edits to big files, and determinism. The existing
  `wide.txt` case in `history_test.go` still gets a real diff, since 25M cells
  is under the budget.
  - **Docs:** `_docs/plan.md` has the `413 request too large` sentence in the
  API conventions and the 32-million-cell sentence in the Diff clarification.

  I did not add DB tests for the 413 on create repo, branch, merge or rename.
  Only the register and login cases are tested.

  - `npm test -- --run` exited 0 (142 passed).
  - `npm run build` succeeded.

  The one failing criterion is skip link first, no focus move on first load.
  - **What happens:** under `npm run dev` (React StrictMode) focus lands on
  `<main>` at page load. The first Tab then skips the skip link and goes to the
  "Log in to create a repository" link (on /login, to the first input).
  - **Cause:** StrictMode runs effects twice, so the `first` ref flag in
  `TitleSync` (`App.tsx`) is already false on the second run and focuses main on
  first load.
  - **Production is fine:** in `vite build` plus `vite preview`, focus on load
  is body, the first Tab is the skip link, and Enter focuses `main#main`.
  - **Suggested fix:** compare against the previous pathname instead of a
  boolean flag, and add a StrictMode test.

  Passing browser checks (Chromium):
  - **Titles:** all routes in the issue are correct, including blob, edit, new,
  and unknown routes.
  - **Focus after navigation:** it moves to main after a link click and after
  browser back.
  - **Reduced motion:** with reduce emulation, animation and transition
  durations are 1e-05s everywhere, and the skeleton pulse is off. The control
  run shows 2s and 0.15s.
  - **320px:** no horizontal page scroll in light and dark on home, repo (owner
  view with all buttons), tree, history, commit, compare with a diff, blob,
  edit, and new. A 32-character username is truncated and Log out stays on the
  same row.
  - **Dialog guard:** the browser could not show that focus is not stolen from
  an open dialog, because the dialog unmounted with the page. That is only
  covered by the code check.

  Visual problem, not an acceptance criterion: at 320px the repo page
  latest-commit card wraps the message "init" one letter per line next to the
  long author name. Screenshot:
  /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-akniyetislambekkyzy-Desktop-Assignnment2-webfro
  nt/8e654564-6ef0-4401-8fa5-89c3c6b32520/scratchpad/qa35b_repo_320_light.png.
  The home repo table scrolls inside its own wrapper and clips the description
  column; I judged that acceptable.

  Test repo deleted via the API. Servers stopped. No repo code changed.

  My Playwright scripts are in
  /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-akniyetislambekkyzy-Desktop-Assignnment2-webfro

› Message from general-purpose
  Issue #29 is implemented on branch `issue-29-commit-log-cte`, head `d0fd393`,
  one commit on top of current main. `go test -p 1 -race ./...` passes (exit 0).
  I commented on the issue with the branch, head commit and results. I did not
  push, merge or close.

  - **Store method:** `RepoStore.WalkFirstParents(ctx, tip, limit)` in
  `backend/store.go` returns the first-parent chain in one recursive CTE, newest
  first. It returns `CommitRow{OID, Data, Parent}`.
  - **Parent detection:** the parent comes from the commit header only, using
  the regex anchored on `tree`/`parent` over the first 100 bytes. I used
  `encode(..., 'escape')`, so a non-UTF-8 body cannot fail the query. Go still
  parses each body with `gitobj.ParseCommit`.
  - **Missing objects:** a missing tip or a missing first parent gives an error
  wrapping `ErrNotFound`, so the endpoint answers `500 internal error` rather
  than a shortened log.
  - **`listCommits`:** `backend/history.go` now calls
  `WalkFirstParents(skip+51)` instead of loading commits in a loop. The response
  shape is unchanged, including `has_more` and empty pages. The `1<<40` early
  return is unchanged.
  - **Tests:** two tests at the end of `backend/history_test.go`:
    - `TestCommitLogStatementCount` counts statements with a pgx tracer. Page 1
  and page 3 of the 120-commit repo each issue at most 6.
    - `TestCommitLogHeaderOnly` covers four cases: a root commit whose message
  holds a `parent <oid>` line gives exactly one commit, a merge follows its
  first parent only, and a missing parent gives 500.
  - **Not changed:** `isAncestor` in `merge.go` still loads one commit per
  query. I noted it as a follow-up in the issue comment.

  /auth/me. `Cache-Control: no-store` is on /auth/me and not on /health.

  /auth/me without a cookie.
  - **Scope:** the commit touches only the allowed files, and the existing
  401/404 responses are unchanged.

  **Slow client:** an idle TCP connection was closed after 10.0s, and one
  sending headers a byte at a time was cut off after 11.3s.

  **Timeout review:** the largest commit body is 64 MiB (`maxCommitBody`). It
  fits in the 90s read timeout at about 0.7 MB/s or faster. Other bodies are
  small JSON. Tree and diff responses are bounded by the 1 MiB per-file content
  limit. I did not upload a real 64 MiB commit.

  **Tests:**
  - `gofmt -l` is empty and `go vet` is clean.
  - The config and header tests pass.
  - `go test -p 1 -race -timeout 40m ./...` passed in every package; the root

  re-runs were needed.

  **Live server (port 18432):**
  - Bodies of 1 MiB + 1, both valid-JSON-shaped and pure garbage, return 413
  `request too large` on register, login, POST /repos, create branch, merge and
  rename. A valid JSON object followed by 1.1 MB of trailing junk also returns
  413.
  - Delete branch takes no body, so there is nothing to cap there.
  - Bodies of exactly 1 MiB pass the limit and get a normal 400 validation
  error. Small invalid JSON is still 400 `invalid JSON body`.
  - The branches, merge and rename handlers (which decode quietly) keep their
  order. Oversize bodies get 401 without a cookie, 404 for an unknown repo and
  403 for a non-owner. Only the owner gets 413.
  - A commit with a 900 KiB file (901,049-byte body) returns 201 under its own
  64 MiB limit.
  - I built a commit with three 4000x4000 all-changed files plus a 5000-line
  file with one edit. Commit detail and compare each returned 200 in about 8 s,
  which is mostly Neon latency.
    - `big.txt` got a real 1-hunk diff and `f1.txt` a full diff.
    - `f2.txt` and `f3.txt` came back `truncated: true` with empty hunks and
  zero counts.

  **Extra files (branches.go, merge.go, rename.go):** each has a one-line change
  and I found no regressions. The 404/403 ordering is unchanged, and 400 versus
  413 behaves as intended.

  **DB tests for 413 on the quiet-decoder handlers:** the issue's criteria only
  require DB-free register and login tests, so I did not FAIL for them. I

› Message from general-purpose
  QA verdict for #29: FAIL. I posted it as an issue comment
  (https://github.com/L0nelyG0d/micro-git/issues/29#issuecomment-6219424088).
  Branch issue-29-commit-log-cte, commit d0fd393 (detached checkout).

  **The failure.** `GET /repos/{u}/{repo}/commits?page=1099511627776` (page
  1<<40) returns `500 internal error` on a non-empty repo.
  - On main it returns `200` with `commits: []` and `has_more: false`. The issue
  requires this answer to be unchanged.
  - The server log says `unable to encode 54975581388801 into binary format for
  int4`. The CTE compares `depth` (int4) with the `limit` parameter `$3`, and
  `skip+50+1` overflows int4 for any page above roughly 42.9 million.
  - The `page > 1<<40` guard in `history.go` doesn't help. No test covers a
  large page on a non-empty repo. On an empty repo the answer is 404 before the
  walk, so that case never reaches the query.
  - Suggested fix: cast `$3::bigint` and make `depth` bigint, or clamp in Go.
  Add a regression test with `?page=<1<<40>` on the 120-commit `long` repo.

  **Everything else passes.**
  - gofmt and go vet are clean. The history tests passed, and `go test -p 1
  -race -timeout 40m ./...` exited 0 (backend 813s). I saw no connection flakes.
  - Live comparison: I built main's code (e3ba803) and d0fd393 and ran them on
  ports 18701 and 18702 against the same database. A 125-commit repo, a
  root-only repo and an empty repo gave identical responses on 54 of the 56
  URLs. The two that differ are the page-1<<40 requests on the 125-commit and
  root-only repos.
  - The identical cases covered:
    - pages 1 to 5 (50/50/25/0/0 commits, `has_more`
  true/true/false/false/false);
    - `invalid page` and `ref not found`;
    - ref as a branch and as a commit oid, including a mid-history oid;
    - the empty repo (404) and unknown repo or owner;
    - the same fields and 125 unique commits in order.
  - Adversarial messages gave the same log as main: a `parent <oid>` line, a
  fake tree/parent text, 500 characters, multi-line, tab, backslash, unicode and
  RTL.
  - `TestCommitLogStatementCount` passes (page 1 and page 3 at most 6
  statements). I did not count statements independently beyond the test's
  tracer.
  - A missing first parent gives 500, which matches main and the issue, so it is
  acceptable.
  - The repo page's latest-commit bar uses page 1 of this endpoint, which is
  byte-identical to main. I checked that through the API only, not in a browser.

  I did not exercise non-UTF-8 header bytes directly, because the commit API
  rejects them. The design (`encode(..., 'escape')`) handles them by
  construction.

  I changed no repo code and merged and pushed nothing. Scratch files are in
  `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-akniyetislambekkyzy-Desktop-Assignnment2-webfr

  - The full `go test -p 1 -race ./...` passes, with no flaky failures.

  that the mid-commit-delete test fails without the fix.

  **New file `backend/concurrency_test.go`**
  - `TestConcurrentCommitsSameTip`: 8 goroutines commit to `main` with the same
  `expected_tip`. One gets 201, seven get 409 `branch tip has moved`, each with
  `current_tip` equal to the winner's oid, and the branch ends on the winner's
  oid.
  - `TestConcurrentFirstCommits`: the same race on an empty repository, plus a
  check that the repository ends with exactly one ref.
  - `TestConcurrentRenameSameName`: one rename gets 200, the other gets 409
  `repository already exists`.
  - `TestConcurrentCreateSameBranch`: 8 goroutines `POST /branches` for the same
  new name. One gets 201, seven get 409 `branch already exists`.
  - `TestConcurrentRepoDeletedMidCommit`: the `beforeCommitTx` hook deletes the
  repository after the lookup, and the commit answers 404 `repository not
  found`.

  All five share a small `fireTogether` helper that releases the requests
  through a start channel. They don't use the #30 helpers beyond `testServer`
  and `newUserCookie`.

  **Production changes**
  - `backend/commits.go`: added the `beforeCommitTx` hook (nil in production),
  run after the repo lookup and before the transaction opens. A foreign-key
  violation on the tree-building, commit-object and ref inserts now answers 404
  `repository not found`.
  - `backend/db.go`: added `isForeignKeyViolation` (SQLSTATE `23503`) next to
  `isUniqueViolation`.
  - No schema change, and `backend/branches.go` did not need the same mapping.

  **Existing test:** `TestRegisterConcurrentDuplicate` in `auth_test.go` already
  covers concurrent registration, so I did not duplicate it.

  - In `frontend/src/components/MergeDialog.tsx`, the selects now have

  the full branch name.

  32-character branch name. jsdom has no layout, so it only checks the classes
  and the title, not the 320px rendering.

› Message from general-purpose
  Issue #38 done on branch issue-38-delete-race-404, head b460e6e, based on main
  e39a90b (no rebase needed). I did not push, merge or close the issue.

  I groomed the issue first and posted the acceptance criteria as a comment. I
  posted it after the code was written but before the commit and the handover
  comment. I also commented the handover on the issue.

  What changed (all in /Users/akniyetislambekkyzy/Desktop/Assignnment2-webfront/
  .claude/worktrees/agent-ae15f5376f679e729/backend):
  - **reading.go:** added `repoGone` and `readError`. When a read hits
  object-not-found or context-canceled and the repo no longer exists, it answers
  404 `repository not found`. `resolveRef` does the same when the ref lookup
  fails because the repo is gone, since deleting a repo deletes its refs. A
  missing object in a repo that still exists stays a 500 and is logged.
  - **history.go and compare.go:** the commit list, commit detail and compare
  use `readError`. Commit detail checks that the repo exists before answering
  "commit not found".
  - **httpx.go:** `internalError` no longer logs `context.Canceled` as an error.
  It still writes a 500, but the client has already gone.
  - **handler.go and repos.go:** a nil-by-default `Server.hook` plus `testHook`,
  fired at "repo-loaded" (in `repoFromPath`) and "ref-resolved". The hook is my
  own and does not depend on #33. It also fires for the write endpoints (they
  call `repoFromPath`), and it does nothing unless a test sets it.
  - **delete_race_test.go:** the new tests. Nine hook-driven cases cover blob,
  tree, commits, compare and commit detail, with the repo deleted either before
  the ref lookup or after the ref is resolved. Commit detail has no ref step, so
  it is only tested at "repo-loaded". Each expects 404 `repository not found`.
  One test deletes blob objects from an existing repo and expects a 500 plus an
  error log line. One test checks that a canceled error is not logged while a
  real error is. There are no sleeps. I did not run the new tests against the
  unfixed code to confirm they fail there.

  Test results: the three new tests passed on their own, and the full `go test
  -p 1 -race ./...` passed, including the gitobj, linediff and treebuild
  packages. I made no frontend changes, so I did not run the frontend tests.

  target. All 107 tests passed in both runs, with none skipped or removed. This

  with main. Nothing is pushed. The issue comment is at
  https://github.com/L0nelyG0d/micro-git/issues/30#issuecomment-6220947899 and
  carries the before/after timings.

  - **`backend/auth_test.go`:** `testServer` now uses one pool for the whole
  test binary, created with `sync.Once` on first use and closed after `m.Run` in
  a new `TestMain`. Each test still gets its own `Server`, JWT secret and
  clock, and tests still skip when `TEST_DATABASE_URL` is unset.
  - **bcrypt cost:** `TestMain` sets `bcryptCost = bcrypt.MinCost`. The only
  production change is `var bcryptCost = bcrypt.DefaultCost` in
  `backend/auth.go`, used by `register` in `auth.go` and `dummyHash` in
  `backend/session.go`. Nothing in non-test code ever lowers it. `dummyHash` is
  built at package init, before `TestMain` runs, so it keeps the default cost in
  tests.
  - **`backend/history_test.go`:** `newHistFixture` computes the 120 commits in
  Go with `gitobj.Hash` and writes them in one `INSERT ... SELECT unnest(...)`.
  The `fifty` and `main` refs are still created. No assertion changed.

  to 38.7 s and `TestCommitLogHugePage` from 58.8 s to 19.9 s.
  `TestCommitErrors` (about 50 s) and `TestCommitDetail` (about 48 s) did not
  change, because their time is the handler's own Neon round trips.

  The `commits_test.go` and `auth_test.go` loops the issue pointed at are single
  HTTP commits or a few goroutines, not long serial database loops, so they
  stay as they are. The issue comment says why.

  Only `auth.go` and `session.go` were touched in production, one line each plus
  the variable, so overlap with #38 should be minimal. I did not run the
  frontend tests, since this change touches only the backend.

❯ check the questions.md file i answered ur questions there

⏺ Agent(PM grooms JWT, OpenAPI, Makefile, CI)
  ⎿  Backgrounded agent (↓ to manage · ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ I read your answers in _docs/questions.md. A PM agent is now creating four
  issues:
  - JWT lifetime of 3 hours
  - openapi.yaml
  - the Makefile
  - CI

  I've noted every answer in the file. Two of your answers need a decision from
  you before I build anything, and I'll explain them below.

  Keycloak
  - What it is: Keycloak is a separate identity server. It would run next to the
    Go backend (it needs Docker or Java), and it would handle logins and issue
    signed tokens.
  - Why I recommend against it: your login already works the way you describe.
    The user logs in once. The server then signs a JWT and puts it in an
    httpOnly cookie, and every later request carries only that cookie, never the
    password. The backend checks the signature each time. Keycloak would add a
    whole extra service, plus a rewrite of register, login and the middleware,
    for no gain here. The one thing it would add is token revocation, and the
    shorter JWT covers most of that.
  - Your call: keep our own JWT (my recommendation), or adopt Keycloak as a
    bigger task.
  - The 3-hour lifetime: it is going ahead either way.

  Rate limiter
  - Why a limiter matters: bcrypt makes each login attempt cost the server real
    time. Without a limiter, someone can guess passwords all day or flood the
    server with registrations.
  - Two counters on login, in memory:
    - Per account: failed attempts per username. The 5th failure in 15 minutes
      locks that username out for a while, and the wait doubles each repeat.
    - Per IP: a looser limit for all attempts from one address, so one client
      can't try many usernames.
  - The "known user" tiers:
    - Known account: a username that exists gets the strict per-account counter.
    - Unknown username: an attacker guessing names could probe which accounts
      exist. So unknown names get the same counter and the same slow response as
      real ones, tracked by IP. The error stays a flat "invalid username or
      password".
  - Trusted clients: a client that has already logged in successfully (a valid
    session cookie) gets a more generous limit than an anonymous one. Register
    gets a per-IP limit only.
  - What a blocked client sees: a 429 with a Retry-After header and the same
    flat error message.
  - The catch: the counters live in memory, so they reset when the server
    restarts. That's fine for a local project.
  - "Signature check": I'm not sure what you mean by it. Possible meanings:
    a. Verify the JWT signature on every request. We already do.
    b. Sign requests from the frontend with an HMAC, so the server can tell they
       came from our app. That adds little, because a browser can't keep a
       secret.
    c. Sign the failed-attempt counter into a cookie. That keeps the server
       stateless, but anyone can clear the cookie.
  - My recommendation: option 1 plus the in-memory counters above. Tell me which
    you meant, and whether the design is right, and I'll file the issue.

  Pagination
  - How I read your answer: "per page makes no sense because it's not a global
    project" as don't paginate, so the default stays "no".
  - If I read it wrong: tell me and I'll add per_page.

  Everything else is as you said. The openapi.yaml and the Makefile are being
  groomed now.

  CI will run on merge to main, on a throwaway Postgres database so it needs no
  secrets. It will only do repeatable mechanical checks: formatting, go vet,
  tests, the build, spec linting and a secret scan. QA keeps the
  acceptance-criteria, exploratory and browser checks.

⏺ Agent(PM grooms limiter and pagination)
  ⎿  Backgrounded agent (↓ to manage · ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Your lockout concern is right, and I've changed the limiter design to address
  it.

  Rate limiter
  - Per IP and username pair: failed logins are counted per IP address and
    username together, not per username alone. A bot on another address can no
    longer lock out the real user.
  - Per IP: a looser cap on total attempts from one address stops it trying many
    usernames.
  - No hard lockout: a client over the limit gets a growing delay and a 429 with
    a Retry-After header. The wait ends on its own.
  - Known-device cookie: this is how I'd use a signature. A successful login
    sets a signed cookie tied to that username. A request carrying a valid one
    gets the generous limit, so the real user keeps working while a bot hammers
    the account. A forged cookie, or one for a different user, doesn't help.
  - Unknown usernames: they behave exactly like real ones, so nobody can tell
    which accounts exist.
  - Keycloak: we keep our own JWT, with the 3-hour lifetime.

  Pagination
  - page and per_page: I'm adding both to the repo list, and per_page to the
    commit list. The default is 50 and the maximum is 100.
  - Frontend: the repo list and the History page get Previous and Next controls.
  - Contract: the change goes into plan.md and openapi.yaml.
  - Schema change: it may need a database index, which would be a schema change.
    The engineer will flag it before applying it to Neon.

  What's moving
  - A PM agent is writing both as groomed issues.
  - The limiter starts after #40 (the 3-hour JWT) merges, because both edit the
    auth and session files.
  - Pagination starts after #41 (openapi.yaml) merges, because it updates the
    spec.
  - Engineers are still working on #40, #41 and #42, and CI (#43) follows.

  Veto any part of the limiter design before it merges. You can still change the
  numbers too, such as the attempt count and window.

  only to the process that needs it, and never echoed. `.env` is never sourced.
  - **PORT:** it defaults to 8080 and is overridable on the command line. `make`
  does not read `PORT` from `.env`.
  - **Busy ports:** `backend` and `dev` print "port N is busy; run: make
  <target> PORT=<other>" and exit non-zero. `lsof` is checked first, with `nc
  -z` as a fallback.
  - **Port 5173:** `frontend` and `dev` also pre-check it, and Vite runs with
  `--strictPort` so it cannot silently move to another port.
  - **`make dev`:** the two jobs each get their own process group and the shell
  traps INT, TERM and EXIT. It sends TERM to both groups, then KILL after 5
  seconds. If either process exits on its own, it stops the other.
  - **`make test`:** it exports `TEST_DATABASE_URL` and `JWT_SECRET` from `.env`
  when `.env` exists, then runs the commands from the issue.
