  ⎿  Loaded _docs/CLAUDE.md

⏺ I wrote the rate limiter write-up to /Users/akniyetislambekkyzy/Desktop/Assign
  nment2-webfront/_docs/rate-limiter.md. I didn't put anything in settings.json.
  I assumed "the complex part" meant the rate limiter, since it's the only
  complex piece so far. Tell me if you meant something else.

  I skipped settings.json because it only holds configuration such as
  permissions and hooks, not instructions. The agent rules are already in the
  project's markdown docs, which every session loads:
  - Agent workflow rules: _docs/AGENTS.md and _docs/process.md.
  - Role instructions: _docs/team/*.md.
  - Open questions and answers: _docs/questions.md.

  What the limiter is: it slows down password guessing and account spam on login
  and register. It must never let a bot lock out the real user. The code isn't
  written yet; it starts after #40 merges, and the file is marked as design
  only.

  How it works:
  - Counting: failed logins are counted per IP and username together. A bot on
    another IP can't use up the real user's allowance.
  - Penalty: the first 5 failures are free. After that the server answers 429
    with a Retry-After header, and the wait doubles from 1 second up to 15
    minutes. It's a delay, not a lockout.
  - Spraying: a looser cap of 30 failures per IP stops one IP from trying many
    usernames.
  - Known device: a successful login sets a signed cookie, so that user's own
    browser gets 20 free failures. The signature uses a key derived from
    JWT_SECRET, so the cookie can't be forged.
  - Enumeration: unknown usernames get exactly the same treatment as real ones,
    so the limiter doesn't reveal which accounts exist.
  - Register: limited to 10 per IP per window.

  Details:
  - Client identity: only RemoteAddr is used, because X-Forwarded-For is easy to
    fake and the app is local-only.
  - State: everything is in memory and resets on restart, capped at 10,000
    entries.
  - Tests: the clock is injectable, so tests don't need to sleep.
  - Numbers: all the numbers are defaults you can change.

  QA for #40 and #41 is still running, and the #46 engineer is still working.

✻ Sautéed for 16s · done Saturday 11:19 PM

❯ how do i write what u shuold do in every session?                             

⏺ Put the standing instructions in a CLAUDE.md file. Claude Code loads it
  automatically at the start of every session.

  Where to put it
  - Project, shared with the repo: CLAUDE.md in the project root, or
    .claude/CLAUDE.md.
  - Just you, for every project: ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md.
  - Just you, this project, not committed: CLAUDE.local.md in the project root.

  What yours does now. There is already a _docs/CLAUDE.md containing @AGENTS.md.
  The @ imports that file. But Claude only loads CLAUDE.md from the directory
  you start in and its parents, so it may not be picked up when you start from
  the project root. To be sure, create CLAUDE.md in the project root with
  something like:

  @_docs/AGENTS.md
  @_docs/process.md

  You are the controller: launch PM / Analyst / Engineer / QA agents, merge 
  QA-approved
  branches into main, close issues, never write feature code yourself.
  If a decision needs the user and they don't answer within a minute, log it in
  _docs/questions.md with a default and keep working. Never stop working.

  Other options
  - Type # or run /memory to save a note to memory or edit these files from
    inside a session. I also keep a memory folder for things you tell me, such
    as "stash questions when away".
  - Use settings.json for behavior, not instructions. That means permissions,
    hooks and environment variables. For example, a hook can run a command at
    session start.
  - Make a custom command in .claude/commands/name.md (or a skill) for a long
    recipe you only want to run on request. Type /name to use it.

  Keep CLAUDE.md short, because it takes up context in every session. Put the
  details in the _docs/ files and import or link to them.

  I can create the root CLAUDE.md now if you want.

› Message from general-purpose
  Issue #46 is implemented on branch `issue-46-redact-dsn`, head commit 76dbe2f,
  on top of main 832c030, which had not moved, so no rebase was needed. I
  commented the grooming findings and the hand-over on the issue, and I did not
  push or close it.

  **What printed the DSN.** I ran the server with a fake DSN
  (`postgres://fakeuser:FAKEPASS123@nohost.invalid/db`); no real `.env` values
  went into any experiment. Two code paths in `NewPool` in `backend/db.go`
  leaked, and `main.go` printed both through `log.Fatalf`:
  - **Parse failure:** pgx's error embeds the whole DSN. It masks the password
  as `xxxxx` but still shows the user and host.
  - **Ping failure:** pgx's connect error shows the user, database and host.

  `config.go` never prints `DATABASE_URL` or `JWT_SECRET`, so it and `main.go`
  needed no change.

  **The fix.**
  - `NewPool` no longer wraps pgx errors.
  - A parse failure now returns a fixed message that names `DATABASE_URL`.
  - A ping failure returns a fixed message that names `DATABASE_URL` plus an
  error class (SQLSTATE, host name lookup failed, timed out, canceled, network
  error, or connection failed).
  - The exit code is still 1, and I confirmed the new message by running the
  server with a fake DSN.

  **Tests.** The new `backend/db_redact_test.go` covers four bad-DSN cases: bad
  port, bad percent-escape in the password, keyword-style DSN with a bad
  `sslmode`, and an unresolvable host. For each it asserts the returned error
  text and the captured log output contain no fake password, user or host, and
  do name `DATABASE_URL`. It also checks that `LoadConfig` does not echo the JWT
  secret or the DSN.

  **Full suite.** `go test -p 1 -race ./...` passed everywhere except
  `TestCompare`, which failed once with a 500 and "unexpected EOF" from Neon.
  Re-run alone, it passes.

  - [x] Cookie Max-Age=10800. PASS.

  criteria is loose, but there is nothing to fix.

  is the injected clock at login. The decoded token has only the claims `sub`
  and `exp`. There is no `iat` claim, which matches the plan's "claims: sub,
  exp".
  - [x] Token accepted just under 3h and rejected just over, on a write
  endpoint. PASS.

  gives 401 `authentication required`.
    - The commit adds a check that POST /repos with the expired cookie also
  gives 401 `authentication required`.
  - [x] No leftover 7-day references in code. PASS. I grepped the whole

  "7-day", week, 168h and 7d. There are no hits in backend, frontend, README,

  sessionTTL back to `7 * 24 * time.Hour`.

  setup-go v6.0.0 (Go version from `backend/go.mod`, cache on), setup-node

  - **No `make` targets used:** `make lint` and `make build` each need both Go
  and frontend dependencies in one job, so the workflow calls the underlying

  - `GET /repos` takes `page` and `per_page` (default 50, max 100) and combines
  with `?owner=`. It returns `{repos, page, per_page, has_more}`.
  - It uses `LIMIT per_page+1 OFFSET` with no COUNT query. A page past the end,
  or a huge `page` (clamped at 1<<40), gives 200 with `"repos":[]`.
  - Bad values give `400 invalid page` or `400 invalid per_page`. `parsePerPage`
  sits next to `parsePage` in `backend/history.go`, and `commitsPerPage` became
  `defaultPerPage`.
  - Commits accept `per_page` and echo it in the response. Validation order is
  repo (404), then page, then per_page, then ref. The commit log still uses the
  single `WalkFirstParents` query.

  Applying the schema twice on the local DB succeeds.
  - EXPLAIN on the local DB (110 repos): the planner chooses a seq scan plus
  sort on a table that small. With `enable_seqscan=off` it uses an Index Scan on
  `repos_created_idx`. I kept the index and put this in the issue comment.
  - New tests in `backend/repos_test.go` cover `per_page` bounds (1 and 100 ok,
  0 and 101 rejected), `page=0`, a past-the-end page, owner filter plus
  pagination, no skipped or repeated repos, and the 50 vs 51 `has_more`
  boundary.

  validation order including a bad `per_page` on an unknown repo returning 404,
  and `per_page=100&page=2`. I extended the statement-count test with
  `?page=2&per_page=100`; it asserts at most 6 statements for the whole request,
  not exactly one for the walk.
  - I updated the existing `{"repos":[]}` assertions in `repos_test.go` for the
  new response shape.

  single page 1 with `has_more` false.

  - `parsePage` moved to `frontend/src/lib/pagination.ts` and Home and History
  both use it.

  same and its URL is still `?page=N`.
  - `RepoList` is a new type in `frontend/src/lib/repos.ts`.

› Message from general-purpose
  Issue #44 is implemented on branch `issue-44-rate-limiter`, head `8782191`, in
  the worktree `/Users/akniyetislambekkyzy/Desktop/Assignnment2-webfront/.claud
  e/worktrees/eng-44` (created from main 9cc415e). Nothing is pushed, merged or
  closed. I commented on the issue with the branch, head commit and results.

  **Test results**
  - `go test -p 1 -race -timeout 30m ./...` against the local Postgres: exit 0,
  all packages ok.
  - `gofmt -l` is empty, `go vet` is clean, and `go.mod`/`go.sum` are unchanged.
  - Frontend `npm test -- --run`: 146 passed, exit 0. `npm run build` also exits
  0. The worktree had no `node_modules`, so I symlinked the main checkout's
  (git ignores it).

  **What changed**
  - **`backend/ratelimit.go`** (new): the constants block, the limiter, the
  `known_device` cookie helpers, `clientIP`, the `tooMany` writer and the
  register wrapper.
  - **`backend/handler.go` and `backend/session.go`**: the limiter is built in
  `NewServer` and reads `s.Now` at call time. `login` checks the limiter before
  any database or bcrypt work, records failures, clears the pair on success, and
  sets `known_device`. Logout clears both cookies.
  - **Tests**: `backend/ratelimit_test.go` covers every case you listed (bot on
  another IP, valid and forged cookies, unknown vs known usernames, growing
  `Retry-After`, reset on success, memory bound). It also covers the per-IP cap,
  the register limit, `X-Forwarded-For` being ignored, case-insensitive
  usernames and bad bodies not being counted, and a many-goroutine race test.
  - **Frontend**: `ApiError` now carries `retryAfter` from the `Retry-After`
  header, and the auth form shows "Too many attempts. Please wait N
  seconds/minutes and try again." on 429. I added a test in `App.test.tsx`.
  - **Docs**: `_docs/plan.md` has a new "Rate limiting" section plus the
  register, login and logout rows and Known limits. The README limit is
  replaced, and `_docs/rate-limiter.md` status is updated.

  **openapi.yaml** is not on main (issue #41 is still in QA). Whoever merges
  second must add to the spec:
  - the 429 response on `POST /auth/login` and `POST /auth/register`, with a
  `Retry-After` integer-seconds header and an `Error` body;
  - the `known_device` `Set-Cookie` on the login 200.

  **Where I picked a default**
  - A login pair is throttled once its failure count reaches 5, so the 6th
  attempt waits 1s, then 2s, 4s and so on.
  - The per-IP cap triggers when the count exceeds 30.
  - Failures from a request with a valid `known_device` cookie count on the pair
  but not on the IP counter, and that request is not bound by the IP cap.
  - Throttled attempts are not counted as failures.
  - Counters are created only on recorded failures or register attempts, so a
  throttled client cannot grow the map.
  - Beyond the 10,000-entry cap the oldest entry is evicted. Expired entries are
  dropped on access and a few per insert.
  - Register counts every request, including bad bodies, in a fixed 15-minute
  window.
  - A burst of simultaneous wrong-password logins can pass the check before any
  failure is recorded, because check and record are separate steps. I left it
  that way for simplicity.

  The last commit message says `Closes #44`, per the repo's commit convention.
  The issue itself is still open.

› Message from general-purpose
  ## QA: PASS (with defects to decide on) for #44, branch issue-44-rate-limiter
  @ 1bc25db

  Checked in my own worktree, since removed, on scratch DBs on local Postgres
  54329 (both dropped). No Neon, nothing committed, pushed or merged, no secrets
  printed.

  The branch is based on 7eb4a5a. main has since moved to 61f6362 (the #43 CI
  merge). A trial `git merge-tree` against it is clean.

  ### Gates
  - gofmt -l: empty, exit 0.
  - go vet: exit 0.
  - go build: exit 0.
  - `go test -p 1 -race -timeout 30m ./...` against the local DB: exit 0. The
  backend package took 54 s; gitobj, linediff and treebuild passed too. This
  includes the openapi sync tests and 22 new ratelimit tests.
  - Frontend `npm test -- --run`: exit 0.
  - Frontend `npm run build`: exit 0.

  ### Real server and curl
  Everything below ran against a real server on localhost:8744 with unique
  usernames and fake passwords.
  - **Throttle:** 5 wrong passwords gave 401. The 6th gave 429 with
  `Retry-After: 1`. After waiting, the next wrong password gave 401 and the one
  after gave 429 with `Retry-After: 2`, then 4.
  - **Correct password during the throttle:** 429, as designed. It is answered
  before any database or bcrypt work (about 0.4 ms against about 65 ms).
  - **Success reset:** a successful login clears the pair's failures.
  - **Enumeration:** an existing and an unknown username give the same status,
  body and headers (Date excluded) for all 6 requests, and the same timing
  class.
  - **Other usernames:** user B from the same IP was unaffected by A's throttle.
  - **Bad JSON on login:** 8 bad-JSON requests were not counted.
  - **known_device cookie:**
    - The cookie is set on login and decodes to `username|unix` plus a
  signature.
    - A valid own cookie gave 20 wrong passwords as 401, then 429 on the 21st. A
  real user with 3 typos then the right password got 200.
    - Tampered signature, tampered payload, another user's cookie and garbage
  all behaved as no cookie: 5 free attempts, then 429.
    - Logout clears both `session` and `known_device` (`Max-Age=0`).
  - **Spray:** after 30 failures across different usernames, the 31st and later
  got 429. A real user with a valid cookie still logged in (200). The same user
  without a cookie got 429, which is by design.
  - **X-Forwarded-For and X-Real-IP:** rotating them did not help; the throttle
  still hit on the 6th attempt.
  - **Register:** 10 requests gave 201, then 429 with `Retry-After: 900`.
  - **Code read:** the cookie key is `HMAC(JWT_SECRET, "known-device-v1")`, so
  the raw secret is not used as a key and does not leak. Throttled requests
  create no map entries. The clock is injectable. The cap is 10,000 entries with
  oldest evicted.

  ### Mutations on scratch copies
  All three were caught by the tests:
  - Keying per username instead of per (IP, username):
  TestLimiterPairsAreIndependent and TestAttackerOnAnotherIPDoesNotBlockVictim
  failed.
  - Skipping HMAC verification: TestKnownDeviceCookie and
  TestForgedForeignAndExpiredCookiesGetNoLeniency failed.
  - Lenient tier ignoring the cookie: TestLimiterKnownTier and
  TestKnownDeviceKeepsRealUserWorking failed.

  ### Frontend in real Chromium
  I ran Playwright against vite with the proxy pointed at the scratch backend.
  - The 429 shows "Too many attempts. Please wait 1 second and try again." (and
  "2 seconds" on the next one).
  - The message is in `<p role="alert">`, and the password field is cleared.
  - Logging in with the right password after waiting got through to the home
  page.
  - The console shows no JS errors, only the browser's own "Failed to load
  resource" lines for the expected 401 and 429 responses.

  ### Docs
  plan.md, README, `_docs/rate-limiter.md` and openapi.yaml (429 response with
  `Retry-After`, `known_device` cookie on login, both cookies cleared on logout)
  match the behavior I saw. The README known-limits line was updated. The
  openapi sync tests pass.

  ### Defects and judgments (none breaks the stated key requirement)
  1. **Check and record are separate (the engineer's own note), and it is
  exploitable.** A burst of concurrent wrong logins all pass the check before
  any is recorded.
     - Measured: 20 simultaneous requests all returned 401, and 100 simultaneous
  returned 23 401s then 77 429s, against a limit of 5. The bypass is bounded by
  how many requests arrive within about one bcrypt time (roughly 65 ms).
     - The pair counter then jumps to a large value, which gives a 15-minute
  throttle. A bot gets tens of extra guesses per window instead of 5.
     - A same-IP bot bursting 20 or more wrong passwords on the victim's
  username could push the shared pair counter past the lenient tier of 20 and
  throttle the real user, even one with a cookie, for up to 15 minutes. A bot on
  another IP cannot.
     - Severity: low to medium. Suggested fix: reserve or increment the counter
  atomically at check time and undo it on success.
  2. **Register counts every attempt, including bad JSON.** Bad-JSON `{bad`
  bodies to register consumed the 10-per-IP budget: 9 returned 400, then 429,
  and a valid register was refused. The docs say "every attempt counts", so this
  is documented, but it contradicts the "bad JSON not counted" expectation for
  register. Login does skip bad bodies. Severity: low. Either move the limiter
  after body parse or accept and keep it documented.
  3. **Design limits, as documented:**
     - A bot on the same IP can throttle the real user's cookie-less login (new
  browser) through the pair and the 30-failure IP cap, up to 15 minutes.
     - Register is IP-wide: 10 requests from a bot block real sign-ups from that
  IP for 15 minutes.

  Verdict: PASS on the stated criteria. The orchestrator or user should decide
  whether defect 1 (and optionally 2) is fixed before merge or filed as a
  follow-up.
  ⎿  Resuming agent a671328

⏺ Agent "QA issue 44 rate limiter" finished · 4m 20s

⏺ QA passed #44 on the stated criteria, but I'm holding the merge. QA found a
  real flaw in the limiter. A burst of simultaneous wrong logins all gets past
  the 5-attempt check before any failure is recorded. In QA's test, 20
  simultaneous requests all got through, and 100 simultaneous requests let 23
  through. A bot on the same IP could use this to push the shared counter past
  the 20-failure limit. Even the real user with a valid known-device cookie
  would then be throttled for up to 15 minutes. That breaks your main
  requirement, so I sent the engineer back to make the check atomic and add
  burst tests. QA will re-check the fix.

  Everything else QA tried held up:
  - Backoff: the 6th wrong attempt gets 429 and the wait doubles each time.
  - Known-device cookie: a tampered or forged one gets no extra attempts.
  - No account enumeration: the same status, body and headers for known and
    unknown usernames.
  - Header spoofing: rotating X-Forwarded-For doesn't help a bot.
  - Frontend: the 429 message shows in a real browser.

  Two things remain by design. A bot on the same IP can still throttle a login
  that has no cookie, and registration is capped per IP.

  Separately, the #45 re-check and the first backend CI run on GitHub are still
  going.

› Message from general-purpose
  The burst defect is fixed on `issue-44-rate-limiter`. The new head is
  `5abb255`, rebased on main 61f6362. Nothing is pushed or merged, and the issue
  is still open. Worktree: `/Users/akniyetislambekkyzy/Desktop/Assignnment2-web
  front/.claude/worktrees/eng-44`.

  **The fix, in `backend/ratelimit.go` and `backend/session.go`**
  - **Atomic check:** the new `loginAttempt` checks and reserves under one lock.
  An admitted request is counted before the password is checked. A request
  refused with 429 changes nothing, so refused requests never grow a counter or
  the map. Bad-JSON and empty-body requests never reach the limiter.
  - **Undo:** the count is taken back on success (`loginSucceeded`, which also
  clears the pair) and on server errors (`loginAborted`).
  - **Separate tiers:** pair counters are now split into a cookie-less tier and
  a known-device tier. A bot's burst on the same IP and username cannot use up
  the budget of a real user holding a valid cookie.
  - **Burst cap:** a burst cannot push a counter above what the same number of
  sequential attempts would reach.

  **New tests in `ratelimit_test.go`**
  - 100 concurrent attempts admit exactly the free count for both tiers (5
  cookie-less, 20 known-device).
  - A 500-request burst leaves the strict counter at 5.
  - A bot burst followed by a cookie-holding real user still gets a 200.
  - Release on abort and on success.
  - The same burst over HTTP, with 100 goroutines against the real handler.

  I rewrote a few older unit tests whose semantics changed: the delay cap is now
  checked through `delay()`, and the known-tier test now expects the two tiers
  to be independent.

  **Docs:** `_docs/plan.md` and `_docs/rate-limiter.md` describe the atomic
  check and the separate tiers. `openapi.yaml` needed no change because its
  wording is still accurate.

  **Remaining limit (documented in `plan.md`):** a real user without the cookie
  (a new browser) who shares IP and username with a bot shares the cookie-less
  counter with it, so that user waits like the bot until the delay passes.

  **Results**
  - **Backend:** `go test -p 1 -race -timeout 30m ./...` on the local Postgres
  exits 0, all packages ok.
  - **Lint:** gofmt is empty and vet exits 0.
  - **Frontend:** `npm test -- --run` exits 0 (146 tests) and `npm run build`
  exits 0.

  I updated the issue comment with the new head and these results.

  ## QA: PASS (issue #44, branch `issue-44-rate-limiter`, head 5abb255)

  I checked in my own detached worktree `.claude/worktrees/qa-44b`. I used
  scratch DBs on local Postgres 54329, since dropped, and never touched Neon.
  The worktree is clean. I changed nothing, merged nothing and pushed nothing.

  **Commands (exit codes):**
  - `gofmt -l .`: empty.
  - `go vet ./...`: 0.
  - `go build`: 0.
  - `go test -p 1 -race -timeout 30m ./...`: 0. All 4 packages ok; the backend
  package took 53.8s.
  - Frontend `npm test -- --run`: 0.
  - Frontend `npm run build`: 0.

  **Live checks against a real built server** (curl and xargs, one fresh server
  per experiment, real bcrypt):
  - [x] **20 simultaneous wrong logins, one user, no cookie:** exactly 5x401 and
  15x429. Last time all 20 got 401.
  - [x] **100 simultaneous wrong logins:** exactly 5x401 and 95x429. Last time
  it was 23x401 and 77x429.
  - [x] **Bot burst, then the real user:** after a 100-request burst (5x401,
  95x429) from the same IP, the real user logging in with a valid `known_device`
  cookie got 200.
  - [x] **Cookie holder tier is 20.** 20 wrong logins with the cookie gave 401.
  The 21st wrong login and a correct login at that point both got 429. A
  concurrent burst of 40 with the cookie gave 20x401 and 20x429.
  - [x] **Success resets.** After 4 failures, a success, then 8 wrong logins,
  exactly 5 got 401.
  - [x] **Refused requests don't grow state.** After 5 failures I sent 300
  refused requests across 20 parallel clients. After 1.1s the next attempt was
  401, so the delay was not extended.
  - [x] **Throttle delays are 1s, 2s and 4s.** Retry-After was 1, then 4. A
  request at 1.1s was admitted (401). The next was refused at +1.2s and admitted
  at +2.2s. The one after that was refused at 3.5s and admitted at 4.3s.
  - [x] **Unknown vs existing username:** identical status codes, bodies and
  Retry-After on every one of 6 attempts. The right password for an existing
  user while throttled also gives the same 429 body.
  - [x] **Forged cookie gets no leniency.** All gave 5x401 and 15x429: a bad
  signature, garbage, and a genuine cookie for another user. A genuine cookie
  for the right user gave 20x401 and 20x429.
  - [x] **Register is limited to 10 per IP.** 10x201, then 429s, even with a
  changing X-Forwarded-For.
  - [x] **X-Forwarded-For, X-Real-IP and Forwarded are ignored.** The 6th
  attempt with a new XFF still got 429.
  - [x] **Bad JSON is not counted.** After 12 bad-JSON and 12 empty-field
  requests, the next 5 wrong logins got 401 and the 6th got 429.
  - [x] **Per-IP cap across usernames.** 31 distinct usernames got 401, then 429
  for the 32nd and 33rd, and for any fresh name. A cookie holder was unaffected
  (200).

  **Concurrent success with failures:**
  - 1 correct plus 3 wrong simultaneously, 10 trials: the correct login got 200
  every time and the failures got 401. The success was never wrongly refused or
  counted.
  - 30 wrong plus 30 correct simultaneously: only about 5 were admitted and the
  rest got 429, so there are no extra attempts.

  **Code read of `backend/ratelimit.go`:**
  - One mutex covers check and reserve, so there is no race window.
  - No path takes the lock twice, so no deadlock.
  - `release` removes an entry when its count reaches 0 or less, so counters
  never go negative.
  - Throttled requests never call `touch`, so they don't grow the map.
  - Entries are capped at 10,000 with LRU eviction and a sweep of expired ones.
  - The undo cannot be abused. The only way to get extra attempts is to succeed
  in the same IP and username pair, which needs the real password. The success
  also wipes any in-flight failure reservations for that pair, but only for that
  pair.
  - A success from the cookie-less tier clears both tiers' pair entries for that
  IP and username. With a shared IP and a correct password, that is by design.
  - Minor theoretical edge: if an IP-wide entry expired or was evicted
  mid-request and was recreated, the undo could decrement another request's
  count. This is negligible and bounded by the 10,000-entry cap.

  **Mutation checks on scratch copies** (the relevant tests failed in each):
  - **Record after, no reserve at check:** `TestConcurrentBurstOverHTTP` failed
  with "89 of 100 simultaneous wrong logins reached the password check, want 5".
  Several limiter unit tests failed too.
  - **Tiers merged:** `TestLimiterKnownTier`,
  `TestLimiterBotBurstDoesNotTouchKnownBudget` and
  `TestLimiterBurstAdmitsOnlyFreeCount` failed.
  - **Skip the undo on success:** `TestLimiterAbortAndSuccessReleaseReservation`
  failed.

  **Browser (Chromium via pw28 playwright, with Vite proxying to the real
  server):** the 6th wrong login showed "Too many attempts. Please wait 1 second
  and try again." Right after that, a correct login showed "...wait 2
  seconds...". After the wait, a correct login redirected to `/` as the
  logged-in user. The password field was cleared after each failure, and there
  was one `role=alert` element. The only console errors were the expected 401
  and 429 resource-load lines.

  **Docs:** `_docs/plan.md`, `_docs/rate-limiter.md`, `openapi.yaml` and
  `README.md` all match the behaviour I observed. That covers the atomic reserve
  and undo, the separate cookie tier, the `known_device` cookie, the 429 and
  Retry-After response, and logout clearing both cookies.

  **Merge preview:** `git merge-tree --write-tree main 5abb255` (main at
  9a4c1f6) exits 1, with result tree 90a9c5c. There are two content conflicts,
  both docs only. `backend/*`, `openapi.yaml` and `frontend/src/App.test.tsx`

✻ Cogitated for 1h 6m 49s · done Saturday 11:55 PM

❯ how do i start the project to cehck what u did                                

⏺ From the project folder, run make dev. I haven't run it myself in this reply,
  but QA ran it on a fresh clone and it worked.

  cd ~/Desktop/Assignnment2-webfront
  make dev

  What it does: it starts the Go backend on port 8080 and the Vite frontend
  together. Press Ctrl-C to stop both. If port 8080 is busy, use make dev 
  PORT=8081.

  Open: the URL Vite prints in the terminal, usually http://localhost:5173.

  Setup:
  - make setup installs the dependencies.
  - make check-env checks your .env. You need DATABASE_URL pointing at Neon and
    a JWT_SECRET of at least 32 characters.
  - make schema applies the database tables.
  - make help lists every target.

  Things to try:
  1. Register and log in. The session cookie now lasts 3 hours.
  2. Log in with a wrong password 6 times in a row. The sixth gets a "Too many
     attempts. Please wait 1 second…" message, and the wait doubles on each
     further failure.
  3. Create more than 50 repos, or use ?page=2 on the home page, to see Previous
     and Next.
  4. The API contract is openapi.yaml. You can paste it into
     https://editor.swagger.io to browse it.

  Tests: make test-fast is the quick run, and make test is the full suite. The
  backend tests are skipped unless TEST_DATABASE_URL is set. The fast local
  Postgres I used for QA only exists in this session's scratchpad, so it may be
  gone. Your .env has a Neon test URL that works, but the run takes about 14
  minutes.

⏺ Port 8080 is held by a process called budgetcon, which looks like the old
  BudgetControl project's server. It isn't ours. I didn't stop it, since it may
  be something you use.

  Run micro git on another port:

  make dev PORT=8081

  Then open the URL Vite prints. The Makefile points the frontend proxy at the
  port you pass.

  If you want 8080 back, stop the old server yourself with kill 98339, then run
  make dev.
